    B40-S1 implementation contract:

    • Slice status: implemented_contract_only.
    • Scope implemented: B40-DVL-M01..M04, B40-DVL-M25, B40-DVL-M28, B40-DVL-M31, B40-DVL-M32.
    • Implementation timestamp: 09-06-2026 17:42 Europe/Moscow.
    • Source context used: output/doubt-technique-comparative-report.md, output/doubt-verifier-loop-batch-task-matrix.md, current Product-Agent OS handoff fields, runtime route contracts, source policy, status snapshot evidence boundaries, task-matrix completion-report rules, Archimedes read-only docs/source audit and Turing read-only runtime/contract audit.
    • Agent and skill routing: Product Manager owns promise, non-goals and UX budget; Architect owns schema, taxonomy, trigger levels and route boundaries; Tester owns future gate/eval acceptance; Reviewer owns false-pass, false-rewrite, fake-evidence and approval-risk checks; Docs Maintainer owns source/backlog/registry transfer; Release Operator owns no-unapproved-actions closure. implementation-planner, agent-team-designer, test-eval-designer, risk-reviewer and docs-maintainer remain the named skills for future slices. Explorer subagents are sidecar audit only and do not authorize edits, tools, runtime actions or completion claims.
    • Product promise: before a high-risk completion claim, the system extracts requirements and claims, checks current available evidence, then returns pass, revise, needs_more_context, blocked_by_approval or accepted_risk with a concise user-visible reason.
    • Non-goals: no unconditional self-reflection rewrite, no hidden external/provider/browser/live server/git/canonical graph/hook/automation action, no durable rule or automation promotion before manual pilots and metrics, no raw private chat logs, no hidden chain-of-thought storage, no secrets in packets, and no generated dashboard/site/project graph as proof by itself.
    • doubt_verification_packet_v1 fields: packet_id, schema_version, trigger, original_request_ref, artifact_under_review, artifact_type, source_context, claim_ledger, requirement_ledger, evidence_ledger, approval_boundary_hits, verdict, revision_budget, residual_risks, next_safe_action, handoff_projection and redaction_status.
    • Claim statuses: supported, unsupported, contradicted, needs_fresh_check, assumption, accepted_gap.
    • Requirement statuses: covered, missing, partial, not_applicable, accepted_gap.
    • Evidence statuses: available, missing, stale, approval_required, not_applicable, accepted_gap.
    • Verdicts: pass, revise, needs_more_context, blocked_by_approval, accepted_risk, blocked.
    • Claim types: factual, temporal, code_behavior, architecture, product, security, release, eval_quality, approval, completion_claim, ux_frontend, data_privacy, source_policy.
    • Artifact types: code, docs, research, architecture, product, review, release, eval, security, data, frontend_runtime, backend_api, plugin_skill.
    • Severity levels: blocker, high, medium, low, note.
    • Confidence levels: evidence_backed, inferred, assumption, speculative.
    • Freshness levels: current, stale, needs_fresh_check, not_temporal.
    • Trigger levels: skip, lightweight, recommended, required, approval_blocked, manual_only.
    • Explicit triggers: user asks to check again, asks whether anything was missed, asks for before-final audit or asks to audit the last answer.
    • Implicit triggers: completion/readiness claims such as done, implemented, works, ready, tests pass, release/readiness recommendations and task-row implementation claims.
    • High-risk work classes: code edits, source-sensitive/current-fact docs, architecture/spec work, security/privacy/release work, AI/eval/RAG work, long-running goals, plugin/skill/automation work and any claim that requires tool evidence.
    • Exclusions: deterministic read-only command output, short explanations without readiness claims, low-risk formatting, owner-explicit no-extra-verification instruction and approval-blocked checks where the next action is to ask or stop rather than improvise.
    • Runtime surfaces: Codex App, CLI, IDE extension, codex exec, subagent and skill. Agents SDK, MCP guardrails, hooks and automation remain future candidate designs only.
    • Runtime compatibility rule: future completion_skeptic_recommendation must be additive inside route_packet, not a new top-level build_result field and not an unknown key inside methodology_packet; otherwise existing strict runner schema v1 and methodology packet validation can regress.
    • Worker contract: produce the artifact plus a short ledger of request coverage, sources/checks used, assumptions, risks and completion-claim status.
    • Skeptic contract: extract requirements and claims, map each to evidence or accepted gaps, identify approval boundaries, and output the packet. The skeptic does not rewrite first, does not invent tool evidence and does not escalate to live/provider/browser/git/canonical actions without exact approval.
    • Reviser contract: change only confirmed gaps or unsupported high-risk claims. Speculative improvements remain optional risks. The reviser preserves source boundaries and writes a concise final summary with residual risks.
    • Escalation ladder: pass, revise, ask_smallest_blocking_question, run_allowed_check, request_approval, accepted_risk, blocked.
    • Revision budget: maximum two revise loops by default; one blocking question at a time; stop on approval boundary, secret/private-data risk, missing source-of-truth evidence, unavailable tools or repeated unsupported claim.
    • UX and cost budget: use a lightweight pass for low/medium risk, a full pass for high-risk completion claims, keep user-visible output summary-first, keep detailed packets as companion evidence, and record skip reasons instead of silently omitting the gate.
    • Handoff projection: packet fields map to existing verified_claims, unsupported_claims, commands_run, verification_evidence, risks, next_agent_task and stop_reason fields. Schema enforcement, route code, validators, templates and skill implementation are delivered by B40-S2 below; eval hardening remains B40-S3 work.
    • No-unapproved-actions proof for S1: no plugin/runtime code was changed, no completion-skeptic skill was created, no artifact template or validator was added, no live server/browser/provider/live external model/API call ran, no target workspace was mutated, no canonical graph source changed, no hook/automation/MCP/Agents SDK runner was activated and no git stage/commit/push/PR was performed.
    • Current verification evidence for this slice: python -m unittest tests.product_agent_os.test_product_agent_os_docs -v ran 76 tests with OK; python tools\check_docs.py returned OK: documentation structure checks passed; git diff --check exited 0. Strict MkDocs is not routed for S1 because no MkDocs navigation, Mermaid diagram, Markdown extension, localized nav, search/sitemap or generated site/ evidence changes are part of this slice.
    • Residual risks: B40-S1 did not prove product usefulness, runtime behavior, validator enforcement, route recommendation, transcript quality, fake-evidence resistance, manual-pilot benefit or promotion readiness. B40-S2 and B40-S3 below now deliver plugin foundation plus eval hardening; B40-S4 through B40-S5 still own docs/site, pilots, final metrics review and closure.
    • Next slice status: B40-S2 and B40-S3 have since been executed below; the next Batch 40 slice is B40-S4 docs/site and examples.

    Risk Register¶

    Risk Mitigation
    The plugin asks too many questions and stalls the user. Enforce one blocking question in first response and max eight total questions.
    HTML dashboard becomes stale and misleading. Show generated_at, evidence freshness, inspection-only banner and source digests.
    Dashboard is treated as source of truth. Docs and snapshot policy state JSON/Markdown control-plane is source of truth.
    Dashboard leaks local paths or private data. Reuse runtime redaction and add dashboard-specific tests for path/secret leakage.
    Existing project dashboard files are overwritten. Preserve unmarked files as companion proposals and use staged-first merge flow.
    Question answers become hidden memory. Store answers only in explicit ledger artifacts, never user-level memory.
    New runtime fields break schema-v1 compatibility. Add fields only inside existing route packet surfaces and validate bounded shape.
    Self-verification is mistaken for approval to rewrite agents, skills or harness policy. Stage scorecards and update candidates only; require owner approval, rollback target and current evidence before any later apply flow.
    Metrics overfit to recently implemented tasks and miss regressions. Keep holdout, prior-failure, synthetic regression and flaky-case groups separate; require raw numerator/denominator counts and confidence evidence.
    Agent/skill evidence leaks private traces or local user paths. Reuse redaction gates before dataset, ledger, report or dashboard generation and block scoring when redaction fails.
    Metrics-loop automation is activated before the manual workflow is stable. Ship daily and weekly automation files as candidate templates only and require automation_manual_stability_present plus explicit approval before promotion.
    Docs-only or simple maintenance work triggers agentic modernization overhead. Add discovery false-positive fixtures and keep agent_skill_metrics_loop applicable only when real agent, skill, subagent, router/tool policy, automation candidate or harness surfaces are detected.
    Code graph overwhelms the browser or agent context on large projects. Use graph summaries, chunked JSON/JSONL, clustering, top-N subgraphs, hidden labels and query packets for 1k, 5k and 10k node cases.
    Code graph implies false dependency precision. Require confidence labels, unsupported-language statuses and tests that distinguish AST/static evidence from inferred edges.
    Static analysis invents cross-file call edges in ambiguous code. Prefer import-guided resolution, emit inferred global calls only on single-candidate matches and record ambiguous calls as explicit uncertainty.
    Incremental cache reports stale topology as fresh. Store manifest freshness with separate AST/semantic hashes, changed/unchanged/added/deleted file sets and graph freshness status.
    Failed scans silently replace a rich graph with a much smaller artifact. Add shrink guard and stage-local backups before overwrite; require explicit acceptance for suspicious graph collapse.
    Agent graph queries expand through high-degree hub nodes and waste context. Add token-budgeted BFS/DFS traversal, hub suppression, context filters, seed scoring and truncation metadata.
    Graph artifacts leak source code, secrets or local user paths. Exclude raw file contents by default, keep line spans/hashes only, reuse redaction policy and block secret-like values in renderer/query output.
    HTML graph is treated as source of truth. Keep code/docs plus graph JSON/JSONL as source/evidence layers; HTML remains generated cockpit review output.
    Graph visualization libraries introduce network or supply-chain risk. Use owner-selected Cosmos.gl only as locally bundled, version-recorded assets with no CDN; keep Graphology-compatible payloads as internal interchange, Sigma.js as fallback/compatibility-only, and preserve a static/table fallback renderer.
    Cockpit navigation hides important warnings after page grouping. Preserve every detailed section exactly once, add local section navigation and test Source Conflicts, Verification Gates and Security/Risk visibility.
    Eight-page cockpit shell becomes a second documentation site instead of a project map. Keep pages action-oriented: source links, graph packets, stale flags, verification gates, approval boundaries and proposal lifecycle must remain visible.
